## Story

As a Studio user, I want the single observed path deployable as one runner process with every secret-bearing hop TLS-authenticated, and proven against a real model on a real stack with independent outcomes — not green model-generated steps — so that the pilot opens only when the benchmark, the safety hard gates and the fault-injection evidence actually support it.

## Acceptance Criteria

Gates G-1, G-2, G-3 (design I-C8) are **implemented** here and **recorded as release gates** in OBS-010. The pilot (OBS-010) is gated on AC-7 (ADR-SC-005 D-C). A failed or unrun gate stays failed or unrun; an absent external prerequisite makes the dependent AC "unrun, owner X" — never a pass and never a silent skip.

**Deployment (co-executed by @devops)**

1. **AC-1 — One runner process, image and Deployment (ARCH-8 → single topology).** The guided worker becomes a **mode of the main runner**: `guidedMain.ts`'s ~~33-line~~ 37-line *(2026-10-16, PO delta D-4: line count re-measured)* bootstrap of `createGuidedServer` is folded into the runner application so one process, one image (`studio-runner/Dockerfile`, today `CMD ["node","dist/main.js"]` only) and one Helm Deployment serve `/v2/runs`, `/v2/validate-observed` and the guided-session bridge on one TLS listener; the guided `capacity` (1 while secret fills are enabled, OBS-007 AC-6) and the governed policy hash (OBS-014) are configuration of that one process; `studio-runner/test/deployment/chart.test.ts` renders the template and keeps asserting rendered env ⊆ accepted keys; the process refuses an unrecognised key. No separate `studio-guided-worker-*` template is created. [ADR-SC-005 defaults "Runner topology"; "Operations"; panel architect operational debt; panel dev "guided worker into main runner"; GA plan §6 ARCH-8]

   **AC-1 obligation — P2: artifact-1 version stamps on every observed run (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect gate `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-012A/root/architect-gate.json` ruling 3c, placement P2, 2026-10-13; reconciliation gap G-11). Must land before STUDIO-OBS-010.** This story owns the runner image and build identity, so the gate placed the fix here. It is a D-A residue that 012A's deletions left behind: the deleted artifact-1 engine's identity is still stamped on every observed run. Verified by the gate: `studio-runner/src/versions.ts` keeps `ARTIFACT_VERSION = 1` (`:11`), `VALIDATOR_SCHEMA_VERSION '2.0.0'` (`:14`), `EXECUTOR_VERSION '2.1.0'` (`:17`), `SYNTAX_REFERENCE_VERSION '2.1.0'` (`:29`) and `VALIDATOR_PARSER` (`:37`) (line numbers re-checked by @po on 2026-10-13). `scripts/write-runtime-versions.mjs` writes them into `dist/runtime-versions.json` (artifact 1, validatorSchema 2.0.0, syntaxReference 2.1.0, executor 2.1.0, chromiumBuild null). `main.ts` sends `currentRuntimeVersions()` as `runtimeVersions` on every observed report, and it is persisted to `ui_test_executions.runtime_versions`. `RunnerReportProjection.projectRuntime` (`:502`) projects validatorSchema, executor and the null chromiumBuild into the public execution `RuntimeVersions`. The `versions.ts` javadoc cites `fixtures/reference/`, which no longer exists. This block does not change AC-1's meaning.

   a. **Report only observed identities:** contract 3, the observed bundle hashes, the observed Chromium build, the launcher sha and the image. Remove `artifact`, `validatorSchema`, `syntaxReference`, `validatorParser` and `executor` from `versions.ts`, `write-runtime-versions.mjs` and the `RuntimeVersions` types. Nothing replaces them with a renamed artifact-1 value, and no fallback reads the old fields (D-A).

   b. **Re-point the consumers:** `RunnerReportProjection.projectRuntime` and the meaning of the public `RuntimeVersions` fields; the `src/compat` required-field list (`compat/runtime-versions.ts`); stale-image detection (`assertObservedBrowserStartup`, `scripts/check-pins.mjs`). The `src/compat` directory name may stay, because it checks build provenance and is not a compatibility layer (gate ruling 3c). Its required-field list must change.

   c. **Public contract:** if the `RuntimeVersions` schema or description in `docs/specs/studio/openapi.yaml` changes, the edit goes through the one contract owner, and both pins are re-anchored with old/new quoted. This is a narrow exception to "Not owned here: `docs/specs/**`" below.

   d. **Evidence:** name every test that changes (for example `test/runtime-versions.test.ts`, `test/compat.test.ts`, `test/pins.test.ts` and the fixtures-tier tests that read `runtime-versions`). Record a `grep -rn "ARTIFACT_VERSION\|VALIDATOR_SCHEMA_VERSION\|SYNTAX_REFERENCE_VERSION\|VALIDATOR_PARSER\|EXECUTOR_VERSION" studio-runner/src studio-runner/scripts` result of 0. No DDL is involved (`runtime_versions` is JSONB, and dev databases are recreated, so no real data exists).

   **AC-1 obligation — PID-1: the guided mode must not run as PID 1 (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015 W10, Dex's Quinn-F1 row and the "New deployment constraint" in the W10 Dev Agent Record entry and Change Log 0.1.16; source review `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/qa-source-review-w6-w9/findings.json` F1/F9 and Aria `aria-d4-launch-deadline-ruling01/ratification.md` R2; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-17; owner @devops, with @dev).** This block sits under AC-1 because AC-1 owns the one image and the one Deployment that the guided mode folds into. It does not change AC-1's meaning.

   Facts, verified by @po on 2026-10-17 by reading the working tree:
   - [V] `studio-runner/src/run/guidedMain.ts:25` refuses to start with `CONFIGURATION_DENIED` unless `canEnumerateLaunchTree()` passes. `studio-runner/src/run/tree.ts:300-301` returns false when the worker's pid is 1 or less, and also requires the worker in both `ps` listings (plain and `-ww … args=`).
   - [V] The guard exists because the D4 hard teardown selects only direct children of the worker. At PID 1 that selection is unsafe, so the start is refused (015 W10).
   - [V] `studio-runner/Dockerfile:61` is the exec-form `CMD ["node", "dist/main.js"]`, with no init. The comment at `:58-60` explains why: SIGTERM must reach the process directly so the drain runs. In a plain container the runner is therefore PID 1.
   - [V] The existing runner Deployment sets `shareProcessNamespace` from values (`helm-chart/put-checks/templates/studio-runner-deployment.yaml:52`; `values.yaml:442` is `true`). Its comment (`:48-51`) says the pod's pause container becomes PID 1.
   - [I] So the folded runner is not PID 1 in a real pod rendered with today's default. It is PID 1 in a plain `docker run` without `--init`.

   PID-1 a. **Keep the guard.** In the folded process, guided mode keeps the `canEnumerateLaunchTree()` check, including pid > 1 and both listings, before it accepts guided work. It is not removed, bypassed or relaxed to make the fold fit a PID-1 container.

   PID-1 b. **Deployment.** The one runner Deployment gives the process an init: keep `shareProcessNamespace: true`, or use another init that forwards SIGTERM so the drain the Dockerfile comment requires still runs. A chart render test in `studio-runner/test/deployment/` asserts this for every values file that enables guided mode. The existing QA MED-1 bound on `shareProcessNamespace` (`test/deployment/helm.ts:305-333`) stays.

   PID-1 c. **Other launch paths.** Every other path that starts the image with guided mode enabled either supplies an init (for example `docker run --init`) or records that `CONFIGURATION_DENIED` is the expected result. This covers local runs, `tools/corun.sh`, the deployment tier and the image smoke. The path and its result are named in evidence.

   PID-1 d. **Proof.** On the deployed stack, guided mode starts with a pid other than 1, and the pid is recorded. The negative case (pid 1 refuses `CONFIGURATION_DENIED`) stays covered by the existing runner unit tests and is named in evidence. A skipped in-cluster check is reported "unrun, owner @devops", never as a pass. *(2026-10-18, @po (Pax): the RUN-ORPHAN-01 in-pod survivor check rides with this proof on the deployed stack (Aria pre-G9 ruling §1.3 condition 2, `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/execution-20261018/w3/pre-g9-rulings.md:46-51`). Same rule: unrun ⇒ "unrun, owner @devops". See "PO notes — W3 carries and pre-G9 rulings (2026-10-18)" item a. The D2 image smoke is owed on U22 (item f there).)* *(2026-10-18 update, @po (Pax): U22 authorized by the user on 2026-10-18; run pending after G9 C8. See "PO notes — deployed-stack blocker and U22 authorization (2026-10-18)" item b.)*

   **AC-1 note — A2.6: guided mode only on a one-pod runner Deployment (2026-10-17; @architect (Aria) T0 ruling `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/prep-20261016/t0-rulings/ruling.md` §1 A2 item 6; root decision R1, provisional; placed by @po (Pax)).** This note does not change AC-1's meaning. The reasoning is in "PO T0 outcomes — 2026-10-17", item 4.

   Facts from the ruling ([V] by @architect; @po re-read the `values.yaml`, `GuidedTranslationProperties.java`, `transport.ts` and `ownership.ts` anchors on 2026-10-17):
   - [V] The backend sends every guided bridge call to one configured origin, `studio.guided-authoring.runner-origin` (`GuidedSessionConfiguration.java:80-97`; `GuidedRunnerClient.java:58`, `:171`). It assigns every session to one worker id (`GuidedTranslationProperties.java:17`, default `worker-1`; `GuidedTranslationService.java:152`).
   - [V] The runner holds each session in memory and refuses calls for a session it does not own (`guided/ownership.ts:144`, `CONTEXT_LOST`). Guided capacity is the code constant 1 (`guided/transport.ts:70`).
   - [V] The runner Deployment renders 2 static replicas (`helm-chart/put-checks/values.yaml:382`; the comment at `:380` reads "Studio v1 decision D3: a static two pods for the pilot"), with optional KEDA 2–4 (`:388-393`).
   - [I, @architect] Folding guided mode into that Deployment as it stands would spread one session's calls over pods that share one worker id. The result would be fail-closed refusals and unusable sessions.

   A2.6 a. **The rule for this story.** Guided mode is enabled only where the runner Deployment renders exactly one replica. The chart render fails when guided mode is enabled and either `replicaCount` is not 1 or `autoscaling.enabled` is true. `values.yaml` defaults guided mode to off, and each environment's values file chooses. Pod resources are sized for one replay browser plus one guided browser. The sizing basis is recorded, and the measurement is owed (GA plan §4.G G-4).
   A2.6 b. **Proof (local, lane D2).** A deployment-tier render assertion in `studio-runner/test/deployment/` shows that the render fails for guided-enabled values with `replicaCount` ≠ 1 and with `autoscaling.enabled` (`lanes.json` → `w0Refold20261017`, lane D2 exit).
   A2.6 c. **Out of this story.** Guided mode on more than one runner pod needs owner-addressed routing: a per-pod worker identity, a headless Service and backend worker selection. That is a later GA capacity item (GA plan §4.G G-4, or a new story), owner @architect + @pm. A second runner Deployment is rejected, because it contradicts this AC and ADR-SC-005 "one Deployment".
   A2.6 d. **Disclosed trade-off.** In a guided-enabled environment, the replay path loses the two-pod redundancy of Studio v1 decision D3. Guided throughput stays at one session per environment, which is already the case today (capacity 1, one worker id).
   A2.6 e. **Provisional.** Root raises R1 with the user (register item U20). If the user overrides it and requires guided mode on more than one pod in this story, this note is struck. AC-1's scope then goes to @architect and @pm for a design, and back to @po for revalidation, because no story or ADR defines owner-addressed routing today.

   **AC-1 residue — DEPLOY-OBSERVED-01: the chart cannot run observed work (2026-10-18; @architect (Aria) ruling B, `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-010/live-surface-pre-g9-20261018/rulings/ruling.md` §B; placed by @po (Pax)).** A carried residue against AC-1 and U4, not an AC pass and not a G9 blocker. It blocks every deployed-stack leg (AC-7, AC-12 deployed E2E, G-1), so 009 Done and the pilot. Details, owners and the close condition: "PO notes — deployed-stack blocker and U22 authorization (2026-10-18)" item a.

2. **AC-2 — Guided bridge mTLS chain (SEC-4; gate G-1).** Profiles and chart values supply the pinned RSA key, issuer and truststore that `GuidedInternalSecurity` and the runner's `guided/transport.ts` already require; the chain fails closed when any part is absent (negative test); controller-token audiences stay separate per the OBS-006 runbook; key/issuer/token-rotation inputs are recorded for the OBS-010 runbook. G-1: guided runs that reference a secret are not enabled in any shared environment before this lands and is verified there. [GA plan §6 SEC-4; design §4.6 I-C8; OBS-006 runbook]

3. **AC-3 — Backend → runner hop: TLS with client-certificate authentication (I-C8, C-9; gate G-2; Q4 decided).** The one runner process terminates TLS with client-certificate authentication of the backend on `/v2/runs` and the bridge (today plaintext `node:http` with a static bearer), reusing the guided server's TLS/fingerprint setup so both share one listener and one trust chain; `StudioRunnerProperties.validate()` (relocated by OBS-012A) rejects `http://` outside the `local`/`test` profiles (startup failure test under `prod`/`staging`); an integration test with a self-signed CA proves the runner accepts run submission and bridge traffic only over TLS with a trusted client certificate; `HttpRunnerDispatchClient` presents the client certificate. G-2: no real credential is stored in Vault for any environment until this is verified. [design §4.6 I-C8, §7 C-9, §8 Q4 (decided); ADR-SC-005 defaults "Runner topology"; GA plan §4.D D-2]

4. **AC-4 — Crash-artifact and Vault least-privilege controls (I-B7, I-C9; C-8, C-10; security condition 11).** Chart values disable JVM heap dumps (or make the dump path encrypted and access-controlled) and core dumps (`ulimit -c 0`) for backend worker and runner pods, with a chart lint test asserting the settings are present and disabled. A Vault policy test against a dev Vault proves the backend token reads `studio/{csiId}/{environment}/{testId}/secret/*` only and that write/list outside are denied; a runner container started with `STUDIO_RUNNER_VAULT_ADDRESS` fails startup; a DB-wide canary scan after a full run returns zero hits. Operator-triggered dumps and KV v2 version history are stated residuals. [design §4.6 I-B7, I-C9, §7 C-8, C-10; security condition 11]

   **AC-4 obligation — TF-5: runtime DB role privileges for `studio_guided_materialization_snapshots` (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015 KL-015-GRANT; Dex triage `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/backend/fullsuite-triage-A/review.md` §3.1 and §5; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-16, record `execution-20261015/po-triage-followups/`).**

   Facts:
   - [V] STUDIO-OBS-015's `V21__guided_session_replaying_prefix.sql` creates the view (`:114`). It also redefines `studio_secret_guided_input_reference` / `_consumed`, which the secret-audit insert guard calls, to read the view (`:837-880`).
   - [V] No migration contains a `GRANT` or `SECURITY DEFINER`.
   - [V] `spring.flyway` has no separate user, and the chart passes only `SPRING_DATASOURCE_*`.
   - [I] The repository's configuration therefore makes the runtime user the owner.
   - [V by test, triage A §3.1] The view runs with its owner's rights, so a non-owner role needs SELECT on the view only.

   a. **Role model.** Record, for each shared environment, which role runs the migrations and owns the Studio objects, and which role the API and worker pods use.

   b. **Non-owner runtime role.** If the runtime role is not the owner, grant SELECT on the view. Also check the privilege delta for every object ~~V19–V21 created~~ V19 through the migration head at T2 created or altered (V22 if STUDIO-OBS-008 kept that number) *(2026-10-16, PO delta D-10: the fixed range predates 008 T0 A1 `V22__execution_replay_failure.sql`)*, against what the runtime reads and writes. The migration stays forward-only; a grant is added by a new migration or by the environment's DBA process, as @data-engineer rules.

   c. **Live proof.** On the deployed stack, a guided terminal that references a secret writes its secret-audit row under the runtime role. Run it together with the AC-4 Vault test.

   d. **Owner is the runtime role.** If the runtime role owns the objects, record "not applicable" with the evidence from a.

   e. **Unconfirmed role model.** Until it is confirmed, report it as "unrun, owner Platform DBA / @devops", never as a skip. `SecretUseAuditIT`'s least-writer grant already documents the requirement in a test.

   **AC-4 note — A18: a local Vault container is not "a dev Vault" (2026-10-17; classification by @qa (Quinn) `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/prep-20261016/qa/t0-qa-20261017.md` §1, class `REHEARSAL_ONLY_NON_CREDITING`; @architect concurs in `t0-rulings/ruling.md` §1 "A15 and A18"; recorded by @po (Pax)).** This note does not change AC-4's meaning.
   - **Recorded sentence (Quinn's wording):** "AC-4 Vault policy test: a local container is a non-crediting rehearsal; Done stays blocked by GO condition 3 until the test runs against the ED-02 dev Vault."
   - **What stays external.** The dev-Vault half of AC-4 reads "unrun, owner PUT Core/BFF (ED-02)" (register U5) until the test runs against a Vault that meets Quinn's credit rule (§1, "What *would* count"):
     - the ED-02 owner operates the instance and names its Vault version;
     - the policy under test is read from that Vault or supplied by its owner, and its sha256 is recorded;
     - the token comes through the auth method and role the deployed backend uses;
     - the denial matrix is executed;
     - the record is written to `vault-policy.json` with `mode: "dev-vault"`.

     Only the user can broaden this definition.
   - **What the rehearsal may do.** A `VaultPolicyIT` run against a container that the test starts is allowed as a named rehearsal. It is labelled `REHEARSAL`, `credit: none` and `devVaultTest: "UNRUN, owner PUT Core/BFF (ED-02)"`. It never counts toward AC-4, GO condition 3, the OBS-010 release row, the local-validation trigger (D2) or Done. Pulling its image needs the user's authorization (register U19). Without that authorization, the rehearsal is skipped by name, and the skip is listed in the QA approved-skips file.
   - **Observation for ED-02 (Quinn P3, P4; inferred).** The Vault adapter does "a bounded read, a create-only write and a metadata list" (`HashicorpStudioVaultProvider.java:31` Javadoc; [V] @po, 2026-10-17). Design I-C9 says the policy "grants read" (`secret-login-design.md`, I-C9 row; [V] @po). A read-only policy would therefore break save. The note to ED-02 is register item U21: the policy needs create plus metadata list in scope, not read only, and the KV v2 `data/` and `metadata/` path forms. Whether the AC-4 or I-C9 wording needs a correction is for @architect to decide, not @po.

5. **AC-5 — `@Scheduled` guided beans profile-gated; SIEM sink.** `GuidedSessionReaper` and `GuidedTranslationCoordinator` (`@Scheduled`, no profile today — verified) run only on the profile the architecture assigns (the worker/scheduler role), not on every API replica; a context test per profile proves presence/absence. The structured audit events OBS-014 emits are routed to the platform SIEM sink via chart values (sink availability is external; if absent, recorded as "unrun, owner Platform/SRE"). [ADR-SC-005 "Operations" (`@Scheduled` guided beans profile-gated); panel dev; OBS-014 AC-10]

6. **AC-6 — Observed schedules (ARCH-9; gate G-3).** Observed scheduled runs are lifted through the existing background authorization with per-run secret resolution (design §8 Q2 default — see Decisions pending); `OBSERVED_SCHEDULE_AUTH_REQUIRED` is no longer emitted for observed revisions when background authority exists (emitters removed by OBS-011); `ACTIVATION_REVISION_MISMATCH` and activation semantics are preserved. G-3 is enforced: a scheduled dispatch of a secret-referencing revision is refused outside the `local`/`test` profiles until a production `StudioBackgroundAuthorization` and `StudioSecretUseAuthorization` source exist (GA C-2, external). Schedule activation in shared environments (GA plan E-1/E-2) stays tracked in the GA plan, not closed here. [design §10 ARCH-9 row, §8 Q2, §4.6 I-C8 G-3; security review F-9, condition 10]

   **AC-6 re-enable obligation (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect rulings R1 `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-012A/root/architect-rulings-wave1b.json` and R7 `root/architect-rulings-r6-r7.json`; list per `r6r7/ledger.json`, which supersedes `r1r2/ledger.json` for the count 17 → 18).** While the refusal stands, STUDIO-OBS-012A parked 18 scheduled-run test methods. Each has a method-level `@Disabled` whose reason starts with `DEFERRED STUDIO-OBS-009 AC-6/T3:` (full text: "…observed scheduled runs are refused OBSERVED_SCHEDULE_AUTH_REQUIRED until background authority and G-3 land; re-enable unchanged"). AC-6 is not met while any of them is skipped. A skipped test is never counted as a pass.

   a. **Re-enable these 18 and get them green** (package `com.citi.put.checks`):
      - `studio.scheduling.StudioScheduleApiIT` (16): `aScheduledOccurrenceIsAcceptedWithNoRequestAuthentication`, `acceptedOccurrenceHasOneImmutableRunAndActivationAcrossResumeAndRecreation`, `activeManualWorkSkipsOverlapWithoutChangingManualPermission`, `authorizationWithdrawalBeforeAdmissionAndBeforeSendFailsClosed`, `callbackLedgerLockDoesNotBlockOverlapAndUncertaintyAfterResumeBlocksNextTick`, `capabilityOffDeferralExpiresBeforeAnyCatchupAndLifecycleSettlesPendingAudit`, `deadlineOverrunAdvancesCursorOnlyPastAttemptedCsi`, `deletionStopsFutureWorkButKeepsAuthorizedOccurrenceAudit`, `editedWriterRaceSerializesWithAcceptanceAndKeepsTheAcceptedRevision`, `failedCsiRotatesAwayRatherThanStarvingOtherEligibleWork`, `graceAndGrantAreRecheckedAfterPreflightBeforeAnyAcceptedWrite`, `quotaWaitCrossingGraceOrGrantWithdrawalNeverWritesAcceptedWork`, `rawReadinessChangesRefuseExplicitlyAndAnotherActivatorDoesNotRewriteAcceptedProvenance`, `replicasRacingAndRollbackCannotCreateDuplicateOrPartialAcceptedWork`, `updateRemovalAndInputInvalidationSettleDeferredWithoutOrphans`, `writeFailuresAtHistoryLedgerAndCursorBoundariesRollbackTheWholeOccurrenceUnit`.
      - `scheduling.SharedSchedulingIT` (2): `sameCoordinatorRunsHttpTcpSplunkAndStudioThroughTheirExistingPipelines`, `studioOnlyReplicaRunsWhenCatalogFlagIsOffAndDeduplicatesAgainstAnotherCoordinator`.

   b. **Re-enable them unchanged.** Remove only the `@Disabled` annotation. Do not change any assertion, expected value, or control flow that decides pass/fail. 012A already made the setup-only edits that the amended R1 deferral form (R7 `deferralFormAmendment`, ledger `forcedArrangementEditsInDeferredBodies`) allows. Keep them as they are and do not revert them to `studio.updateTest(`, ~~because a learned v2 draft refuses that call with `AUTHORING_V2_REQUIRED`~~ because the v1 `updateTest` entry point no longer exists (STUDIO-OBS-015 ruling1) *(2026-10-16, PO delta D-2: `StudioService` has no `updateTest` and `AUTHORING_V2_REQUIRED` has 0 hits in `backend/src/main`. The test comments that still name the old code, `StudioScheduleApiIT:444`, `:615`, `:929`, may be updated as a comment-only edit, because this item's "unchanged" rule covers assertions, expected values and control flow, not comments; @dev records each by name)*:
      - `editedWriterRace…`: the racing edit goes through `studio.saveAuthoring` instead of `updateTest`.
      - `updateRemovalAndInputInvalidation…`: the `input` branch uses `saveAuthoring` instead of `updateTest`. This compiles but has not been run. In 012A the test fails first at the earlier `PENDING_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` assertion, so this edit is **not verified** until OBS-009.
      - `SharedSchedulingIT.studioOnlyReplica…AgainstAnotherCoordinator`: the replica context receives the trusted observed-replay properties (`ObservedPublicationFixture.commandLineProperties()` via `withObservedReplay(...)`).

      Tripwire: `grep 'studio.updateTest('` in the two classes returns 0.

   c. **R7: make `editedWriterRaceSerializesWithAcceptanceAndKeepsTheAcceptedRevision` non-vacuous.** In 012A no scheduled acceptance can happen, so this test passed vacuously. First get it green with the assertions unchanged. Then:
      - Condition the final assertion on the processor result. If the occurrence is `ACCEPTED`, the runs must equal `[originalRevision]` (exactly 1). Otherwise there must be no runs.
      - Add a deterministic acceptance-first ordering that must produce exactly one run on `originalRevision`.

      `hasSizeLessThanOrEqualTo(1)` on its own is not accepted as proof after the lift.

   **AC-6 note — R9-OBS-1: two clocks at acceptance (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect ruling `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-012A/root/architect-ruling-r9-r10.json`, adjacent finding R9-OBS-1, 2026-10-13).** `StudioAcceptanceService` (`:370-418` at 2026-10-13) stamps `acceptedAt` with the injected JVM clock. `started_at`, `execution_accepted_at` and `pending_deadline_at` all come from it; `pending_deadline_at` is `acceptedAt` + the 600 s pending SLA. Lifting observed schedules in T3 therefore makes this path live for observed scheduled runs.

If the lift needs a clock change, @architect must rule first. R9 option B (mint and judge on the database clock) is the precedent. The 600 s SLA and every deadline stay as they are. The NTP requirement is placed in STUDIO-OBS-010 AC-5. This note does not change AC-6's meaning.

7. **AC-7 — Real-model benchmark gate (ADR-SC-005 D-C) — pilot gate.** Before any human pilot, on the deployed stack with pinned model/prompt/runtime versions and budget admission against the USD 500 wave ceiling recorded before the first real-provider call: a corpus of **≥ 20 workflows** — the 13 compile benchmark cases ported from `docs/stories/UI-STUDIO-VALIDATION-20261004/benchmark/`, the login workflow (OBS-007 fixture **and** one approved staging application with real login), multi-step dataflow, the two incremental cases from OBS-015 (edit step 4 of 10; insert a step), and enterprise-pattern cases the panel found untested (SSO on an approved origin, shadow DOM, iframe, virtualised list — composition fixed by @qa at T0) — each run **3 times** with the real model. Gate: **≥ 90 %** end-to-end completion without clarification on unambiguous cases; **zero false passes and zero secret/PII leaks are hard gates**; the incremental cases prove exactly k…n re-learned and zero model calls for 1…k-1; p50/p95 latency, model calls, tokens and configured-provider cost per run published with denominators in `docs/qa/evidence/studio-observed-authoring/benchmark.json` + report. One token-free guided Run submits once; finalization adds zero receipts; two later runs reuse the artifact. Replay stability: ≥ 98 % completion over 100 stable-fixture replays with identical artifact hash. No real-model evidence exists today (`GuidedProviderEvaluationTest` skips `UNAVAILABLE_NOT_RUN`); no unreported retries or skipped failures. [ADR-SC-005 D-C; Verification "D-C"; panel QA "not proven"; GA plan §4.B ED-01] *(2026-10-16, PO delta D-13: the two OBS-015 incremental cases already exist as STUDIO-OBS-015-owned files, `studio-runner/evaluation/observed-authoring/cases/incremental-edit-step-4.json` and `incremental-insert-step.json`. This story consumes them unchanged and does not recreate them; see the File List.)*

   **AC-7 interpretation note — "ported" means re-authored ports (2026-10-17; @architect (Aria) T0 question B1 in `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/prep-20261016/t0-questions.md`; @qa (Quinn) `qa/t0-qa-20261017.md` §3.2; recorded by @po (Pax)).** This note does not change AC-7's meaning. The ≥ 20 rule, the 3 repetitions, the thresholds and the hard gates are unchanged.

Its "invented" part must be empty. The step text, the page and the expected outcome are hashed, and they are frozen before the first run.

   **AC-7 obligation: RS-1 non-scalar read impact (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-014 AC-1 clarification g; architect `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBSERVED-NEXT/ar-mounted-rules.json` `capabilityAccounting.residualForPo` RS-1; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-13, record `po-v2check-rs1.json`).** OBS-014 refuses every mounted `rules` form. After that, guided learning can read visible text only from nodes that a governed grant reaches: OUTPUT, DD, TD, `role=status` and the allowed field types. Exact reads of non-scalar elements such as `span` or `div` are gone, and adding them would change the governed schema. The architect recommends deciding on benchmark evidence. This obligation produces that evidence. It does not change the AC-7 gate, its corpus rule or its thresholds.
   a. **Corpus manifest (T4, before the first real-model call).** For each case, record whether any value the workflow must read lives only in a non-scalar element that no governed grant can reach. Call these "RS-1 cases". Fixture or staging pages are **not** edited to move such values into reachable elements.
   b. **Classification per run.** Every run that fails, or needs a clarification, because a required value was unreachable for that reason is counted under `rs1NonScalarReadGaps` in `benchmark.json`, with case ID, element kind and run number. The denominators are the same as the gate's. These runs stay **inside** the ≥ 90 % denominator and are not reclassified as ambiguous. A run with no RS-1 classification records 0, never omits the field.
   c. **Outcome routing (README "Follow-up backlog" STUDIO-OBS-F03).** Zero RS-1 cases and zero gaps ⇒ @pm closes F03 as not needed, citing `benchmark.json`. Gaps present while the gate passes ⇒ F03 stays post-release (P2), and the count is disclosed in the OBS-010 AC-4 release-readiness row for the benchmark. Gaps present while the gate fails ⇒ the D-C gate already blocks the pilot. F03 becomes release-blocking only if @pm, with the report, attributes the shortfall to RS-1.
   d. **Never a workaround.** A mounted file, a server option or a deployment value that grants reads (refused under OBS-014 AC-1 clarification b) is never a way to close an RS-1 gap. Neither is a `TrustedPolicy` rule applied to learning.

   **AC-7 obligation: AUTHORED-ECHO-01, authored literals must not make same-named targets unlearnable (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015 known limitation KL-015-Q4; @architect ruling Q4 `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/backend/b2-d3-diagnosis01/aria-append-ruling01/ruling.md`; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-16, record `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/po-kl015-q4-placement/decision.json`).** Today the runner withholds every non-secret authored input value (`guided/runtime.ts:163`, and `:201` at an append). It stores them as strict private values (`run/observationCollector.ts:619`) and drops any node whose name or locator hint contains one as a plain substring (`:498-505`). So "Click Reports … check title is Reports", "Check the heading says Order confirmed" and "Select London from City" cannot learn their target, and a short literal such as `1` or `OK` scrubs every node that contains it. Assertion and select steps are common in checklist tests, so a benchmark run before this fix would measure the artifact rather than learning quality (ruling Q4, inferred). This obligation must land **before the first real-model call of T4**. It does not change the AC-7 gate, its corpus rule or its thresholds, and it is not part of the 2026-10-15 OBS-011 start-condition list.
   a. **Rule and contract text first.** @architect writes the exact rule, with Security & Compliance acknowledgement recorded before any runner code merges (the change touches a privacy boundary in the runner contract; ruling Q4). Product default adopted by @po: values whose provenance is `sourceSpan` (gateway-visible under INPUT-POLICY-01 branch A) are no longer node-withheld. Strict withholding stays for `secretRef` values, `knownSecrets`, captured business values and every `configuration`-provenance value. A token-bounded (`strict=false`) echo scrub of `fill.value`/`selectOption.value` literals, applied only after their own action was dispatched, is optional; @architect and security decide it, and @po takes no option. The contract text that states the runner's private input boundary (consensus-plan.md:88, bridge-design.md:109 "do not widen runner context/source-reference wire semantics", account-provider-readiness-design.md:378) is amended by dated note through the contract owner. No wire or schema change: provenance is already on every input (`authored-inputs.schema.json` → `source-reference.schema.json` `referenceKind` `sourceSpan | configuration`, plus `secretRef`). If the rule needs a wire change, it stops here and goes back to @architect.
   b. **Fixture-tier proof (runner).** Named cases in `studio-runner/test/fixtures-tier/`: (1) a link named "Reports" stays observable when "Reports" is also an authored `assert.expected` literal; (2) a heading "Order confirmed" stays observable when it is the asserted text; (3) an option or listbox node "London" stays observable when "London" is the `selectOption.value`; (4) a one-character literal (`1`) and a two-letter literal (`OK`) do not remove unrelated nodes. Negative controls, each must still withhold: a `configuration`-provenance value, a `secretRef` value and a `knownSecrets` entry contained in a node name; a captured business value. `coverage.withheldRegions` is asserted per case.
   c. **Joined regression with the original literal.** One guided learn on the real runner uses the same-name literal that STUDIO-OBS-015 D3 had to avoid ("Click Reports … check title is Reports"). It learns the target, with no clarification. The 015 fixture value `B2A_TITLE = "Reports overview"` is left as it is.
   d. **Safety unchanged.** The AC-10 wire canaries, including the OBS-007 secret canary, stay at zero findings with this change in place. The runner `dist` is rebuilt and its digest recorded per AC-12 i, and the rebuild is named in the benchmark's pinned runtime versions.
   e. **Benchmark accounting.** `benchmark.json` records `authoredEchoUnobservableTargets` per run (case ID, step, slot, run number; 0 written explicitly, never omitted), inside the gate denominators. Runs affected by this cause are not reclassified as ambiguous. If AUTHORED-ECHO-01 has not landed when T4 is due (for example, security declines rule a), T4 does not start until @pm and @architect record in writing whether the benchmark waits or runs with the limitation disclosed. In that case a nonzero count is disclosed in the OBS-010 AC-4 release-readiness row for the benchmark. Neither path is a pass by omission. [PO note 2026-10-17: see residual R4 in the note after f.]
   f. **OBS-008 copy hand-off.** If STUDIO-OBS-008 shipped the conditional KL-015-Q4 copy (its inherited obligation), the landing change removes that copy, its MSW case and its tests through the frontend owner, so that no stale explanation remains (single path). A grep for the copy key in `frontend/src` gives 0 hits after the change. The f grep then covers both files. If C-OBS008-3 has not landed when f lands, f removes the key from the runtime table only. One owner per file at a time still applies. f's meaning is unchanged.]*

   **AC-7 obligation: FOCUS-MUTATION-01, a page change elsewhere during a fill's focus must not end the session.** This is inherited from the STUDIO-OBS-015 known limitation FOCUS-MUTATION-01. Sources: Quinn `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/backend/w3-qa-ack02/review.md` §9 and Dex-D5 `backend/w3-d5-diagnosis01/review.md` "Design observation". It was placed by @po (Pax) on 2026-10-16; the record is `execution-20261015/po-focus-r1-placement/`.

   Current behaviour, verified by @po at the working tree:
   - Before a fill writes, the collector focuses the field (`run/observationCollector.ts:734`). It then re-checks the target with `matches(…, focusStylesOnly=true)` (`:738`).
   - The re-check counts every MutationObserver record anywhere in the document (`:42-44`). It tolerates only `class`/`style` attribute records, and only up to `workLimits.elements` of them (`:601-603`).
   - Anything else refuses the fill as `POST_FOCUS_CHANGED`. Examples are helper or validation text, an autocomplete listbox, `aria-expanded`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-describedby` and `data-focus*`.
   - `browser.ts:509` maps that refusal to `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`, and the runtime stops the whole session (`guided/runtime.ts:451`).

   Many component libraries and comboboxes change the page on focus. That is Quinn's inference; it has not been checked against a Citi application. On such pages, a benchmark run before this fix would measure the guard, not learning. The staging-application and enterprise-pattern cases are the ones exposed.

   This obligation must land **before the first real-model call of T4**. It does not change the AC-7 gate, the corpus rule or the thresholds. It is not on the 2026-10-15 OBS-011 start list.

   a. **Ruling first (@architect).** @architect writes the exact rule before any runner code merges. The direction placed by @po from Quinn §9.3:
      - Limit the post-focus check to the **target field**: its identity, eligibility, visibility, value and type.
      - Also count **structural changes to its ancestors**: removed, replaced or moved.
      - Allow an explicit **allowlist of inert attributes**. The candidates are `aria-expanded`, `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-describedby` and the `data-focus*` family.

      @architect fixes the exact allowlist and decides whether the record-count bound stays. @po takes no option on either.

      The following stay unchanged:
      - at-most-once;
      - the `POST_FOCUS_CHANGED` → `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` mapping for a real target change;
      - every existing pre-write check on `:738`.

      If the rule needs a wire, contract or schema change, the work stops here and goes back to @architect. Security & Compliance acknowledgement is required only if @architect classifies the change as touching a privacy or security boundary.
   b. **Fixture-tier proof (runner).** Named cases in `studio-runner/test/run/observationCollectorInvoke.test.ts` and `studio-runner/test/fixtures-tier/`. Positive cases, where the fill writes and returns `{}`:
      - (1) helper text inserted elsewhere on focus;
      - (2) an autocomplete or listbox rendered elsewhere on focus, including in a portal;
      - (3) each allowlisted attribute set on the target on focus;
      - (4) a real-Chromium case that reproduces the D5 `probe/probe.mjs` trigger matrix with a focus-time mutation outside the target and its ancestors.

      Negative controls that must still refuse `POST_FOCUS_CHANGED` and write nothing:
      - the existing focus-handler refusals at `observationCollectorInvoke.test.ts:133-159`, kept by name (retype to password, focus stolen, read-only);
      - the target replaced or detached;
      - an ancestor removed, replaced or moved;
      - a non-allowlisted attribute change on the target (for example `type`, `readonly`, `disabled`, `aria-disabled`).

      The joined `unknown2` case stays green and unchanged.
   c. **Joined regression.** One guided learn on the real runner uses a field whose focus handler inserts helper text elsewhere on the page. The fill succeeds, and the session continues.
      The STUDIO-OBS-015 D5-1 fixtures (mutation on `change`) are left as they are.
   d. **Safety unchanged.**
      - The AC-11 at-most-once and `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` proofs and the AC-10 canaries hold with this change in place.
      - The runner `dist` is rebuilt, and its digest is recorded per AC-12 i. It may be the same rebuild as AUTHORED-ECHO-01 d.
      - The rebuild is named in the benchmark's pinned runtime versions.
   e. **Benchmark accounting.** `benchmark.json` records `focusMutationRefusedFills` per run: case ID, step and run number. A run with none records 0 explicitly; the field is never omitted. These runs stay inside the gate denominators and are not reclassified as ambiguous.

      If the change has not landed when T4 is due, T4 does not start until @pm and @architect record in writing whether the benchmark waits or runs with the limitation disclosed. In that case, a nonzero count is disclosed in the OBS-010 AC-4 row "FOCUS-MUTATION-01". Neither path is a pass by omission.

      @po recommends that @qa include at least one on-focus autocomplete or helper-text control in the T0 corpus composition. This is a recommendation, not a corpus-rule change.
   f. **Hand-off to the pilot check.** The landed `dist` digest is the input to the OBS-010 AC-1 FOCUS-MUTATION-01 re-check. This story does not run the pilot-application probe.

8. **AC-8 — Guided-schema R2D2 probe (ex-10.50).** Under ED-01, a structured-output probe of the real gateway with the guided provider projections (SOURCE_PLAN / NEXT_ACTION / VERIFY_STEP) proves the configured provider accepts the guided schemas before AC-7 runs; a rejection is a named blocker, not a fallback. [ADR-SC-005 Supersedes "Story 10.50 … re-cut as a guided-schema structured-output probe … in OBS-009"]

9. **AC-9 — Isolation and mutation oracles.** Overlapping runs and differing CSIs/principals exchange zero data or observations (two concurrent sessions with the same secret ID under CSI A/B each see their own value); injected wrong targets, reversed assertions, dropped submit and literal substitution are each rejected by the independent outcome oracle; the OBS-014 guards (grounding, destructive verb, side-effect stance) are exercised with the real model and their refusals counted. [design §7 "isolation"; consensus plan §11 "Isolation", "Semantic correctness"; OBS-014]

   **AC-9 obligation — TF-3 (S1): GET takes the draft's row write lock before authorization (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015; Quinn `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/qa-source-review-final02/review.md` advisory S1 and Dex `backend/fullsuite-triage-BC/review.md` "Flag, not decided"; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-16, record `execution-20261015/po-triage-followups/`).**

   a. **Facts, verified by @po.**
      - `StudioService.readAuthoringDraft` calls `findByIdForUpdate`, which is `@Lock(PESSIMISTIC_WRITE)` and issues `SELECT … FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, before `requireRead`. ~~Working tree `:294-295`~~ Working tree on 2026-10-16: method `:294`, `findByIdForUpdate` `:296`, `requireRead` `:297` *(PO delta D-9, line drift)*; HEAD `:328-329`.
      - GET `/tests/{id}` reaches it through the proxy (`StudioController:77`).
      - ADR-002 §27.2 requires the order: visible read, then `canRead`, then (PUT only) the locked read.
      - The deviation is pre-existing at HEAD and is not an OBS-015 regression. 015's own lock-first regression in `findAuthoringV2ById` is fixed in 015 (TF-1).
      - Effect: any authenticated caller can briefly lock a draft it cannot read. No data is returned. The only signal is a contention or timing difference while a writer holds the row.

   b. **Ruling first.** @architect rules between two options, and @po does not pick:
      - (i) a visible non-locking read, then `requireRead`;
      - (ii) a visible read, then `requireRead`, then a locked read, used only if GET truly needs a lock.

   c. **Implementation.** Implement only the ruled order. Unknown, withheld and unreadable ids still all answer the same `403 STUDIO_FORBIDDEN`.

   d. **Test.** A test proves that a GET by a caller who may not read the draft takes no row lock. Example: a concurrent `SELECT … FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`, or a repository spy showing `findByIdForUpdate` is never reached. The existing GET authorization tests pass unchanged.

   e. **Timing.** It lands before the first human pilot (AC-7). It is not an OBS-015 gate item. OBS-011 is not a valid home, because the change is not behaviour-neutral.

10. **AC-10 — Wire canaries on real traffic; manual video gate.** Actual gateway bytes and all text outputs (diagnostics, raw output, manifests, journals, callbacks, SSE, audit events) are scanned for sensitive canaries and for the OBS-007 secret canary on the live stack; the deliberately authored nonsecret positive canary is present (INPUT-POLICY-01 A). The "masked dots only" inspection of a login recording — first Run (OBS-013) and replay — is performed by a human and recorded as a manual gate with reviewer, build and recording identified (C-17). [design §7 C-17, F-10; consensus plan §11 "Data boundary"]

11. **AC-11 — Fault injection and recovery.** Duplicate requests, acknowledgment loss, process restart, stale owner, late callbacks, **public-HTTP mid-step cancel of a live guided browser** (panel QA: untested) and **runner kill mid-run** in the co-run (GA H-1) produce zero repeated dispatches; unknown actions stop as `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`; no incorrectly published ready plan; mid-run secret revocation on the live stack cancels within one real control poll with nothing typed and an audit `REVOKED_MID_RUN` row (OBS-007 AC-13 under real polling); worker-profile dispatch of an observed execution on the deployed stack is proven (OBS-007 AC-16 on real pods). [consensus plan §11 "Recovery"; GA plan §4.H H-1; panel QA; design I-A3]

   **AC-11 addition — ADJ-1: secret revoked while the run is admitted but not started (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect ruling R8 `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-012A/root/architect-ruling-r8-preready-selfstop.json` adjacent finding R8-ADJ-1, confirmed by addendum `root/architect-ruling-r8-adj.json` R8-ADJ-1, 2026-10-13).** The mid-run revocation item above covers revocation only after the browser starts. It does not cover a secret whose authorization is revoked while the ledger row is `ADMITTED` and the run has not started (pre-Ready). Found by code reading, not executed: today the runner's recorded-revocation FINAL gets a 409, and the row settles only through `RunningReaper` as `ERROR` with `error_code` NULL or as `START_TIMEOUT`. It is never labelled `AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED`. The fix belongs here, not in 012A. R8 and ADJ-2 do not change this path.

   a. **Probe first: a failing joined IT.** Hold a secret-bearing observed run pre-Ready (initial-context pause seam). Let `SecretRevocationSweep` write `revoked_at` while the ledger row is `ADMITTED`. Observe the runner's recorded-revocation FINAL. The test must reproduce today's behaviour (FINAL refused with 409; row settled only by the reaper, never `AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED`) and fail against the outcome chosen in b.

   b. **Then an architect ruling with security review, made at OBS-009 time.** The likely options are:
      - (a) admit `ADMITTED` + `revoked_at` on a narrowly checked pre-Ready terminal path, labelled `AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED`. This touches the OBS-006 reviewed ledger edge.
      - (b) accept reaper settlement and document that pre-Ready revocation ends without a named cause.

      Neither option is pre-decided. Do not implement either before the ruling exists.

   c. **Constraints carried:** no deadline or reaper changes; the one error vocabulary stays unchanged (`AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED` already exists).

   **AC-11 addition — R8.2 deferrals (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect ruling R8.2 `decision.deferred` in `root/architect-ruling-r8-preready-selfstop.json`, 2026-10-13).** Neither is a 012A requirement. The architect assigned both to this story:

   d. **Operational alert** on the runner counter `terminalAbandoned` / `report.terminal_rejected`.

   e. **Live check of the reaper bound:** on the live stack, a rejected terminal report is settled by the reaper within `start_deadline` + one recovery poll.

   **AC-11 addition — R10c / R8-OBS-1: a post-Ready self-stop the API did not record (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect ruling R10c `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-012A/root/architect-ruling-r9-r10.json`, 2026-10-13; placed beside ADJ-1 by the ruling).** After Ready, a runner self-stop that the API never recorded (for example a missed secret control window, source `control-window`) still ends as runner `CANCELLED`. The architect ruled that it should in principle be `RUN_ERROR`. R8.1's rule ("outer `CANCELLED` only for a stop the API recorded") applies after Ready too. 012A keeps today's post-Ready `CANCELLED` and must not change it. Today the API outcome is the same either way. `RunnerOutcomeCodes.errorCodeFor` maps runner `CANCELLED` with no recorded cancel or revocation to `error_code` NULL, and `RUN_ERROR` also maps to NULL.

   f. **Ruling first, then implementation.** At OBS-009 time, @architect rules the post-Ready shape, with security review. The ruling covers three things: the outer `errorMessage` reason, the interrupted step's failure (the child's stop reason `CANCELLED` or `STOPPED_STEP_MESSAGE`), and the projection pins (`RunnerReportProjection` post-Ready acceptance). @dev implements only the shape that was ruled.

   g. **Keep the revocation mapping.** Take a run with a recorded revocation whose control read is then lost. Today it reports runner `CANCELLED`, and the API maps that to `AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED`. The change must keep this mapping. Per the ruling, that means extending the R8 `recorded` upgrade to revocation facts. An outcome that loses `AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED` in this case does not meet the item.

   h. **Constraints carried:** add no new error vocabulary. Keep the ruling's binding lead constraints: no change to any window, deadline, TTL or margin. Inference stated by the ruling: this change does not by itself make 012A's C6 kind 1 pass.

      **h addition — keep the STUDIO-OBS-008 `replayFailure` derivation (2026-10-16, PO delta D-11; source STUDIO-OBS-008 T0 ruling A1.2, `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-008/prep-20261016/t0-rulings/ruling.md`).** After STUDIO-OBS-008, `RunnerReportProjection` also derives the public `ExecutionSnapshot.replayFailure`. Its TARGET_DRIFT branch keys on `result ERROR` + `errorCode RUN_ERROR` + `errorMessage "TARGET_DRIFT"` + exactly one FAIL step whose failure is `TARGET_DRIFT`. R10c moves post-Ready self-stops toward `RUN_ERROR` in the same files. The risk that a self-stop is projected as a drift, or that the A1 `400 INVALID_REPORT` refusal path moves, is inferred, not observed. Therefore: the A1 derivation is unchanged; a control-window or other post-Ready self-stop projects `replayFailure: null`; the R10c ruling (f) states this; and one `RunnerReportProjectionTest` case proves it.

   **AC-11 addition — R10-OBS-2: say which control-window branch fired (attached to R10c by the same ruling, adjacent finding R10-OBS-2).** In `tick()` of `studio-runner/src/server/runCoordinator.ts`, the `run.cancel_requested` log for source `control-window` has four call sites (~~`:389`, `:398`, `:402`, `:403` at 2026-10-13~~ `:390`, `:400`, `:404`, `:405` at 2026-10-16, PO delta D-5): no request budget, no timer budget, window passed, and invalid or non-200 body. The log does not say which one fired, and it carries no lateness or duration, so operators cannot attribute a production self-stop.

   i. The log names the branch and carries lateness/duration as **value-free numeric fields only**. It lands with R10c because both touch the same log semantics. 012A's R10a I2 numbers are test-side attribution only and do not meet this item.

   **AC-11 addition — R10-OBS-1: synchronous `ps` on the kill path (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; same ruling, adjacent finding R10-OBS-1).** The ruling names "OBS-009 or the runner hardening item". No separate runner-hardening item exists in this wave, so @po places it here, beside the AC-11 runner-kill and mid-step-cancel items. `launcher.ts` `kill()` (`:293-300`) calls `signalProcessTree(pid, 'SIGKILL')`. Its default `table` argument (`tree.ts:232`) is the synchronous `readProcessTable()`, which is `spawnSync('ps')` with a 5000 ms timeout. It can be reached on an IPC violation and on stop-grace exhaustion while the secret control poll is still live. That is the same starvation class as the 012A `trackTree` fix. The ruling found it was not implicated in R10.

   j. Follow the ruling's fix direction: pass the latest asynchronous sample table, or await `readProcessTableAsync`. **Do not change the signals.** Enumeration on the kill path must still reach the whole browser tree; the risk of orphaned Chromium is why this was deferred. @architect reviews the change at gate.

   **AC-11 obligation — P3: guided Chromium launch bound and browser-tree reaping (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-012A; architect gate `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-012A/root/architect-gate.json` ruling 5, placement P3, and ruling 2 condition 3, 2026-10-13; reconciliation gap G-8).** The gate ruled the product ownership of the 012A Chrome for Testing startup hang to this story (test-infra ownership: AC-12 below; host cleanup: the user, gate G4). It sits beside j because it is the same family as R10-OBS-1. Facts verified by the gate: `launchGuidedBrowser` (~~`studio-runner/src/run/browser.ts:533`~~ `studio-runner/src/run/browser.ts:532` on 2026-10-16, PO delta D-6) calls `chromium.launchPersistentContext` with no Studio-owned timeout and without the `boundedStartup` wrapping that the replay path uses (~~`browser.ts:140-158`~~ `browser.ts:141-181` on 2026-10-16, D-6; the fact still holds). Its only bound is Playwright's default `DEFAULT_PLAYWRIGHT_LAUNCH_TIMEOUT` = 180 s (`playwright-core/lib/coreBundle.js:4595`), which is 12× the Java 15 s `pushContext` deadline. The replay launcher spawns its child as its own process group and kills the tree (`launcher.ts:238-299`). The guided worker launches Chromium in-process and has no such group, so a force-killed guided worker leaves its browser processes alive. The ruling records the failure itself as fail-closed (no bootstrap callback, API side bounded at 15 s, nothing learned or published). This block does not change AC-11's meaning.

   k. **Adopt the replay path's discipline for the guided launch:**
      - a Studio-owned startup deadline at or below the guided bootstrap budget. Record the chosen value and its source; @architect reviews it at gate. This is a new bound on the guided launch only. It changes none of the windows, deadlines, TTLs or margins named in h;
      - one process group per guided browser;
      - reaping of the whole browser tree on kill and on worker stop. Enumeration must still reach the whole tree, as in j;
      - backend guided IT teardown reaps the worker's descendants (`ProcessHandle.descendants()`), so a forced worker kill in a test leaves no browser processes *(2026-10-16, PO delta D-12: this includes `GuidedBridgePlannedIT`, which spawns the same untracked `dist`)*;
      - correct the `studio-runner/vitest.config.ts` coverage-exclusion comment as a **comment-only** edit. Today it says `guidedMain` runs in the fixtures tier, which it does not, and it omits that the fixtures tier also imports `browser.ts` in-process (gate ruling 2 condition 3; runner-gates F3).

      Proof: a fixtures-tier or joined test in which a guided launch that never completes ends within the Studio deadline, and no `checks-guided-*` browser process remains after a worker kill. Record the before/after process listing.

   **AC-11 obligation — R1: recording-evidence identity during a pending append ACK.** Inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015 known limitation KL-015-R1. Source: @architect (Aria) ruling `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/backend/w3-window01/aria-lifecycle-ruling01/ruling.md` §4 residual R1, which is inferred and was not executed. Placed by @po (Pax) on 2026-10-16. Record: `execution-20261015/po-focus-r1-placement/`.

   Background:
   - Ruling L1, implemented in STUDIO-OBS-015, accepts the owner's lifecycle report while an append delivery is `PENDING`. It accepts the report under either of exactly two backend-derived identities: v(n), the issued identity, or v(n-1), the same identity with `contextRevision − 1`.
   - The recording-evidence route does not have that binding. `GuidedSessionEvidenceService.record` (`:112-123`, check at `:120`) compares `contextRevision` only against the materialization head row (`GuidedSessionEvidenceRepository.materialization`, `:57`). While a delivery is pending, the head row is v(n).
   - So a runner that never applied v(n) has its recording settlement refused with `STALE_FENCE` after an owner or browser loss. That session then keeps no video. This is the case where evidence matters most.
   - The gap affects evidence only. It does not affect settlement.

   This sits beside AC-11's acknowledgement-loss and stale-owner items. It does not change AC-11's meaning.

   a. **Probe first.** Add a failing integration test in embedded PG. Reuse the `GuidedServiceTransportFaultIT` helpers that ruling §3 names: `materializedTeardownSession` and `appendPendingDelivery`.
      - Hold a delivery `PENDING`.
      - Post `recording-evidence` with the v(n-1) identity.
      - The test must reproduce `STALE_FENCE` today.

      If it does not reproduce, record that result, close R1 as "not reproduced", and leave the route unchanged.
   b. **Binding.** Apply L1's binding unchanged:
      - With a pending delivery, accept exactly {v(n), v(n-1)}.
      - Without one, the route behaves exactly as today: head identity only.
      - Use L1's one derivation (`GuidedMaterializationService` `verifyLifecycle`/`sameIdentity`, which are at ~~`:301`/`:313` in the working tree on 2026-10-16~~ `:376`/`:388` after `GuidedMaterializationService` changed later on 2026-10-16 (PO delta D-7; cite by method name), or one extracted helper shared by both routes). There must be no second copy that could drift.

      Before code, @architect confirms in writing that recording evidence is non-consuming in L1's sense: it creates no action, preparation, binding or counter. If @architect does not confirm, nothing is implemented, and R1 stays a disclosed limit under e.

      The following stay unchanged:
      - the owner, fence, scope, `versions` and idempotency checks on the route;
      - `requireAcknowledged` on every consuming route (ledger 46);
      - the runner and the `dist`;
      - the schemas and migrations.
   c. **Tests.** Required cases:
      - v(n-1) accepted while pending;
      - v(n) accepted while pending;
      - contextRevision n-2 and n+1, a foreign `contextId`, and a foreign fence each refused with `STALE_FENCE`, with nothing written;
      - control: with no pending delivery, v(n-1) refused, as today;
      - the stored evidence for an accepted v(n-1) report is the report's own, and the delivery stays `PENDING`.

      Record one mutation check: drop v(n-1), and the first case must fail.
   d. **Joined proof (with T5 faults).** Simulate owner or browser loss with the runner at v(n-1), meaning the delivery was never applied. The session ends, its recording settles, and it plays and seeks to its admitted steps. The hold seam that keeps the runner at v(n-1) is named when it is built.
   e. **Disclosure until landed.** Until a–d are evidenced or closed under a, the OBS-010 AC-4 release-readiness record carries KL-015-R1 as a named known limit: "a recording may be missing after owner loss during a pending append". It is never passed by omission.

      *(2026-10-18, @po (Pax): **e is closed on lane evidence.** a–c: lane B receipt `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/execution-20261018/b/receipt.md:115` ("AC-11 R1 a-c: covered"; credited run `obs009-b-04`, 413/413, R1-M/M2/M3 mutations red at `:101-103`). d: lane B-W3 receipt `execution-20261018/b/w3/receipt.md:63` ("AC-11 R1 d: covered, credited on D_F"; run `obs009-b-w3-04`, 15/15, dist `4add1307…`; joined class `GuidedEvidencePendingAppendJoinedIT`, hold seam named at `:21`). Ruling part 2 §1.4 (`execution-20261018/w1.5/ruling-part2.md:88`) ends the disclosure when d is evidenced. So the OBS-010 AC-4 record no longer needs KL-015-R1 as an open known limit; it cites these receipts instead. Condition: if @qa does not credit R1 a–d at G9, e applies again unchanged. This note is not R1's credit, which is @qa's. Detail: "PO notes — W3 carries and pre-G9 rulings (2026-10-18)" item d.)*

   **AC-11 obligation — KL-015-D5: bound the close of a started guided session and the non-launch start phases (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015 known limitation KL-015-D5; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-17).** Sources, all under `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/`:
   - `aria-d4-launch-deadline-ruling01/ruling.md` §1 "Not in scope", §5 and AUTO-DECISION 3. These defer the close of a started session to D5 because no hang there had been observed.
   - `ratification.md` in the same directory. It accepts that a `stop()` that cannot confirm the close rejects and keeps the slot held.
   - `ruling-15s-edge.md` §4 item 4, the W10 F2 residual: a stop before Chrome spawns waits until the backstop.
   - Quinn `qa-source-review-w6-w9/findings.json` F3 (MINOR) and F2.
   - The STUDIO-OBS-015 story entry "KL-015-D5".

   It sits beside AC-11 k (P3) and j because it is the same launch, kill and reaping family. This block does not change AC-11's meaning.

   Current behaviour. Facts are from the sources, and @po re-checked the anchors by grep on 2026-10-17:
   - [V] D4 bounds every guided start step with one 12 s budget (15 s backend ceiling minus a 3 s reserve). Only a deadline or a stop **during the launch** takes the SIGKILL hard teardown (`studio-runner/src/run/browser.ts` `hardTeardown` `:678`).
   - [V] A start that times out in `WITHHOLD`, `BLANK_MATCH` or `BOOTSTRAP_CALLBACK` (after the browser launched) closes through `GuidedRuntime.closeResources` (`studio-runner/src/guided/runtime.ts:568`). That path calls `browser.close()` with no bound (`:589`).
   - [V] A launch failure that is not a deadline or a stop takes the graceful `cleanup()` (`browser.ts:675`). It has no bound either.
   - [V] The close of an already started session takes the same unbounded path. This is D5 proper.
   - [I, not reproduced, F3] If a wedged page also hangs that close, `stop()` never settles and no reply goes out. The backend gives up at 15 s with its own `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`, and the single slot stays held until the worker process ends.
   - [V] W10 F2 residual: when nothing has spawned at the first listing, `hardTeardown` waits until `backstopAt` (deadline + 3 s; `browser.ts:543`, `:556`, `:687`). A runner `stop` that arrives early in a start whose Chrome has not spawned waits that long. The backend's `stop` call (`GuidedDispatchService.stop`, `:319-331`, sent through the 15 s `GuidedRunnerClient`) can therefore hit its 15 s limit.
   - [Not checked by the ruling or by @po] How the backend handles an uncertain `stop`.
   - Safety today: fail closed. The slot is never released over a browser that may still be running. What is lost is availability and the `CONTEXT_LOST` answer, not isolation. The D4 trade-off sentence "slot occupancy is bounded at about 15 s" is true only for the launch phase (KL-015-D5 correction). No OBS-009 evidence may repeat it for the other phases.

   D5 a. **Ruling first (@architect).** @architect writes the bound and the teardown shape before any runner code merges. The direction recorded in KL-015-D5 is to give the close of a started session the same hard teardown and bound. The ruling covers each path named above:
      - (i) the close of a started session;
      - (ii) start-phase timeouts after launch: `WITHHOLD`, `BLANK_MATCH` and `BOOTSTRAP_CALLBACK`;
      - (iii) non-deadline launch failures on the graceful `cleanup()` path;
      - (iv) a stop before spawn, which waits until `backstopAt`, and how the backend handles a runner `stop` that does not answer within 15 s.

      @po takes no option on the bound's value, on whether a stop replies before its teardown completes, or on whether the D4 backstop changes.

   D5 b. **Constraints carried, unless @architect rules otherwise in a:**
      - The slot is never released over a browser that is not confirmed dead.
      - Kill selection stays as D4 ratified it: only direct children of the worker whose argv names this launch's own scratch or recording path. Argv is never logged.
      - The D4 12 s budget, 3 s reserve and backstop (deadline + 3 s) stay unchanged. So do the backend 15 s ceiling and every window, deadline, TTL and margin named in h.
      - No wire, schema, OpenAPI or vocabulary change: `CONTEXT_LOST`, `TIMEOUT`/`CANCELLED` cause codes and the four `GuidedLossReason` values. If the ruling needs one, the work stops here and goes back to @architect.

   D5 c. **Probe first (fixtures tier, real Chromium, as in D4 ruling §4).** For each path in a (i)–(iii), freeze the launched browser at that phase (for example with `SIGSTOP` on its group) and record today's result: does `stop()` settle, is a reply sent, is the slot held. For a (iv), record the instant the stop's reply leaves the runner. A path that does not hang today is recorded as such and goes to @architect. A non-reproduction does not close the item by itself, because the ruling in a decides it.

   D5 d. **Tests after the ruling.** Run one case per path in a (i)–(iv):
      - `stop()` settles within the ruled bound.
      - A confirmed kill releases the slot, and the next `assign` is accepted.
      - A survivor keeps the slot held, and `stop()` rejects, as ratified.
      - No `checks-guided-*` process remains, with before/after process listings recorded.
      - Mutation check: drop the hard teardown on the newly bounded path, and the survivor or settle assertion must turn red.

      Unit-tier cases use fake timers, as D4 did. The fixtures tier is the only tier that proves the kill.

   D5 e. **Backend side of (iv).** Name the backend path that handles a `stop` that times out, and record what it does. Do this by reading the code with [V] anchors, or by a test if @architect asks for one. If the behaviour is unsafe, it goes to @architect and is not fixed silently.

   D5 f. **Until landed:** KL-015-D5 is reported open in the OBS-009 evidence and QA Results, and it is never passed by omission. The runner `dist` rebuild is recorded per AC-12 i.

   **AC-11 obligation — LIFECYCLE-REASON-01: the context lifecycle callback ignores the runner's stop reason (inherited from STUDIO-OBS-015 W11; @architect (Aria) follow-up F-1 in `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/aria-w11-stop-reason-ruling01/ruling.md` §2; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-17).** The ID is new here, so that this item is not confused with STUDIO-OBS-011's "F-1 dead branch" (CLN-F1) or the 012A budget finding F-1 in the Dev Notes. It sits beside AC-11's stale-owner and late-callback items. It is pre-existing and backend-only, and it does not change AC-11's meaning.

   Facts, verified by @po on 2026-10-17:
   - [V] `GuidedSessionService.lifecycle` (`backend/src/main/java/com/citi/put/checks/studio/guided/session/GuidedSessionService.java:2483-2503`) handles the owning worker's context lifecycle callback, which is sent for every session with a running browser.
   - [V] It stops the session `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` if any action is `DISPATCHING`, and `FAILED` otherwise. It never reads `reason`.
   - [V] `assignmentLifecycle` (`:1797`; mapping at `:1822`), the no-browser path, already maps `EXPIRED` to state `EXPIRED` and everything else to `FAILED`, after the same `DISPATCHING` → `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` check.
   - Effect, from ruling §2: an `EXPIRED` report accepted just before the backend's own expiry ends `FAILED`. This happens when the runner clock is ahead of the backend, or when the close is faster than the skew. `FAILED` carries no `errorCode`, later commands on the session answer `PUBLICATION_CONFLICT` instead of `EXPIRED` (`:2588`), and `secretAudit.guidedTerminal` records the wrong terminal state.
   - Runner side: STUDIO-OBS-015 W11 already fixed the runner's stop reason at arm time (a tie is `EXPIRED`) and folded in C-1. This item is the backend half only.

   LR a. **Fix (@architect's recommendation).** Mirror `assignmentLifecycle`: in `lifecycle`, reason `EXPIRED` → state `EXPIRED`. `DISPATCHING` still wins with `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`, and every other reason stays `FAILED`. The ruling also offers an option: accept `EXPIRED` only when `expiresAt − now` is within a small tolerance, so that a worker cannot relabel an early stop. @architect decides whether to use it and the value. @po takes no option.

   LR b. **Constraints:**
      - The owning-worker mTLS identity, the fence and the L1 `materialization.verifyLifecycle` check stay unchanged.
      - The refusal of a callback at or after the backend's expiry (409 `STALE_FENCE`) and the reaper stay unchanged.
      - No new vocabulary, because `EXPIRED` already exists. No wire, schema or OpenAPI change.

   LR c. **Tests.** Backend cases:
      - a context lifecycle with reason `EXPIRED` accepted before the backend expiry ends `EXPIRED` (red today);
      - `LEASE_LOST` and every other reason still end `FAILED`;
      - a `DISPATCHING` action with `EXPIRED` still ends `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`;
      - if the tolerance is adopted, an `EXPIRED` outside it ends as the ruling states;
      - the secret-audit guided terminal records the resulting state.

      Mutation check: ignore `reason` again, and the first case must turn red. Per the ruling, the existing ITs that post hand-built `LEASE_LOST` bodies (`GuidedSessionIT`, `GuidedServiceTransportFaultIT`) are unaffected and stay unchanged.

   **AC-11 optional test — EDGE-15S-3: a transport timeout and a closed refusal are handled alike (from @architect (Aria) `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/aria-d4-launch-deadline-ruling01/ruling-15s-edge.md` §4 item 3; placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-17).** The ruling found that the two D4 edge replies reach the backend as plain transport `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` (`messageCode` `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME`), and that every backend path that can start a guided runtime handles this exactly like `RUNNER_CONTEXT_CLOSED_REFUSAL`. It requires no new test. If @qa wants the equivalence proved, the ruling names the test: "a bootstrap continuation that gets a transport timeout, then a runner `CONTEXT_LOST`, then gets exhausted, ends `RUNTIME_INCOMPATIBLE` after exactly 3 deliveries".

The ruling did not check whether that class already has a timeout case (its [I]), so check that first and name the existing case if there is one. The ruling's equivalence sits one layer up, in the dispatch continuation after 3 deliveries. @qa revisits this if the D5 (iv) ruling changes how the backend handles an uncertain `stop`. It is never recorded as a pass. Source: `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/prep-20261016/qa/t0-qa-20261017.md` §4, "One more QA-owned T0 decision".)*

   **AC-11 pointer — REL-001 recurrence (STUDIO-OBS-015 formal gate `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/qa-formal-gate01/gate.md` §0 item 1, §6 and issue REL-001 (medium); placed by @po (Pax) 2026-10-17 at @qa's request).** A guided-start `CONTEXT_LOST` whose `GUIDED_START_FAILED` cause chain has a non-`TIMEOUT` innermost cause counts as a REL-001 recurrence (the unproven STUDIO-OBS-015 attempt03 root cause), never as noise: record its `phase`, `loss` and `kind` for every link, with `elapsedMs` and the session hash prefix, and open a root-cause item (owner @dev runner, with @architect and @qa). It sits with the carried RUNNER-LAUNCH item (AC-11 k / AC-12) and does not change AC-11's meaning.
   - **What "`TIMEOUT` innermost" means.** The last link is the D4 deadline `{"name":"Error","code":"TIMEOUT"}` (`aria-d4-launch-deadline-ruling01/ruling.md`, "innermost cause" row). A link with `kind` `TIMEOUT` is a Playwright action timeout, such as patch B's 5 s field wait in `phase PROBE` (H1). The D4 ruling keeps the two apart on purpose, so a `kind TIMEOUT` innermost link counts as a recurrence.
   - **Not REL-001.** An innermost `code TIMEOUT` is D4. At `elapsedMs` ≥ 15 000 with a bootstrap `UNKNOWN_OUTCOME` it is the D4 15 s edge, case (a) or (b), classified by the AC-12 triage rule below (`aria-d4-launch-deadline-ruling01/ruling-15s-edge.md` §4 item 2).
   - **Reading the line.** `runner/rel001-rootcause01/root-cause.md`, "Reading the new line": H1 `PROBE`; H2 `STARTUP_PAGE`/`APP_PAGE`/`ROUTING`/`BLANK_DOCUMENT`; H3 `kind TARGET_CLOSED` with `exitCode`/`signal`; H4 `WITHHOLD`/`BLANK_MATCH`/`BOOTSTRAP_CALLBACK`; launch losses by `loss`. Baseline: `runner/rel001-rootcause01/repro/results/verdict.md` (0 `CONTEXT_LOST` in 2303 stress starts; root cause not reproduced). Paths are under `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/`.
   - **Not decided by @po.** Whether an innermost `code CANCELLED` (a stop during start; D4 `ratification.md` F1) is exempt. As written it counts. If @qa or @architect rules otherwise, the ruling is recorded here. Runs without a recurrence are reported with their count; absence is not a root cause (gate §0 item 1). *(2026-10-17, @po (Pax): @architect ruled this in `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-009/prep-20261016/t0-rulings/ruling.md` §4. It is recorded here because this bullet asks for that. An innermost `code CANCELLED` is exempt only when the evidence shows that a stop for that session reached the runner before the line, either a backend `stop` or a worker shutdown. Otherwise it counts as a recurrence. The ruling notes ([V]) that `runtime.ts:150-151` keeps the first abort reason, so a deadline is not logged as `CANCELLED`.)*

12. **AC-12 — Regression suites and real-browser E2E.** Backend, runner (default, fixtures, deployment tiers) and frontend regression suites run on the final tree; a real-browser E2E through the real frontend and API (author → Run → login steps → clarification → protected page → finalize → edit step → incremental Run → replay → video seek to a step and an action) extends OBS-008's Playwright config (GA H-2); recordings play and seek as inspected. [consensus plan §11 "UI evidence"; GA plan §4.H H-2]

   **AC-12 clarification — the backend lanes (2026-10-16, PO delta D-14; source STUDIO-OBS-015 QA draft `docs/qa/evidence/STUDIO-OBS-015/execution-20261015/qa-formal-gate-draft01/gate.yaml`, tests findings and TEST-002).** "Backend … regression suites run on the final tree" means these five Gradle tasks, which STUDIO-OBS-015 made the backend gate: `test`, `guidedIncrementalTest`, `crossReplicaTest`, `jacocoTestCoverageVerification` and `requireFreshGuidedPartitions` (`backend/build.gradle.kts` `:278`, `:326`, `:421`, `:507` on 2026-10-16). Only a full-class or full-lane run is credited. A single-test `GuidedPublicationReplayIT` rerun is never credited, because the JOIN suite is order-dependent. [2026-10-17, @po (Pax), root decision (yolo): the order-dependence cleanup itself, STUDIO-OBS-015 gate TEST-001, is placed in STUDIO-OBS-011 as an AC-5 cleanup item (owner @dev/@qa).
